 2. A. 04.16.19 
2. RECOGNITION 
A. Mission Moment – Martell Elementary Students Embrace “Character” 
As t his evening’s Mission Moment , we would like to take th e opportunity to 
recognize students from Martell Elementary School for  embracing “Character”, 
one of the six C’s of deep learning.  Three students from Martell are here with 
us this evening to share a presentation they created to show what Character 
means to them. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Invite principal Michelle Barsh up to       
introduce Martell students Anastasia Kulikowska, Anshika Sharm, and 
Riley Durak.    
3
2. B. 04.16.19 
2. RECOGNITION 
B.  Oakland County Outstanding Teacher of the Year Awards 
The Troy School District has many outstanding teachers on its staff. This 
evening we are proud to recognize the accomplishments of 43 individuals 
who were nominated for  the Troy School District Outstanding Teacher 
Award Program.  
The nominees represent exemplary educators at the elementary, middle 
and high school levels. They were nominated by their peers, parents and 
by students. These teachers have had a tremendous impact on the lives 
of so many through their dedication and commitment to their students. 
Their nomination letters each described how the professionals we honor 
here tonight go above  and beyond to ensure that their students learn, 
grow and thrive.  
Would you please come forward as your name is called. (Distribute 
certificates/plants.) 
First, we honor 40 outstanding nominees, who have touched the lives of 
countless students and families in the Troy School District. We are grateful 
for their dedication and passion for student success.

Three of our district nominees were sent to Oakland Schools to represent 
Troy in the county-wide outstanding teacher award program, administered 
by Oakland Schools. 
Zach Rondot , 4th Grade Teacher at Costello Elementary  School, was 
named TSD Elementary School Teacher of the Year and the Oakland County 
Elementary Teacher of the Year. 
Sue Domin, 7th Grade Teacher at Boulan Park Middle School was named 
TSD Middle School Teacher of the Year.   
Josh Pudaloff, Computer Science Teacher at Athens High School  was 
named TSD High School Teacher of the Year AND Overall TSD Teacher of 
the Year. 
At this time, I would like to invite Mr. Rondot, Mrs. Domin and Mr. Pudaloff 
to say a few words.

RECOGNITION  
Mission Moment 
Prudential Spirit of Community 
opportunity to recognize  Amogh Gowda, a junior at Troy High 
School, who was a distinguished finalist for  the Prudential 
Spirit of Community Award  for developing an awareness 
program on organ donation.  Amogh is also a certified 
volunteer for the Gift of Life Michigan and speaks at venues 
and volunteers at comm unity events to empower youth and 
promote the fact that one organ donor can save up to eight 
lives.   
State School of Character: Wass 
Elementary School 
Honorable Mention: Wattles 
Elementary School, and 
Emerging: Athens High School:  
The Board also recognized and honored Wass Elementary School 
for being named a 2019  State School of Character. A select few 
schools across the coun try a re chosen each year as a State 
School of Character through the implementation of 11 core 
principles, which include promoting good character, culture and 
ethics; fostering student self -motivation, giving students the 
opportunity for moral action and en gaging families and 
community members as partners in character- building efforts. 
Wass Elementary School was praised for the high quality of work 
8
Award 
As this evening’s Mission Moment, we would like to take the  
in all 11 principle areas  and the school’s application has now 
been forwarded on  for consideration as a National Sch ool of 
Character, which will be announced later this Spring.   The Board 
also commended Wattles Elementary School for being named a 
2019 Honorable Mention School of Character  and Athens High 
School for being recognized as Emerging.  Ms. Lori Soifer , 
Character.org Coordinator for the State of Michigan , was in 
attendance this evening to present the official certificate to 
Matt Jansen, Principal of Wass Elementary.

It was moved by Dr. Philippa rt and supported by Mrs. Fleming 
that  
WHEREAS, Public Act 435 of 2018 establishes the Office of 
School Safety within the Department of State P olice; the Office 
of School Safety is tasked with working with the Michigan 
Department of Education to create model practices  for school 
safety and offering school safety trainings to school districts; 
WHEREAS, Public Act 538 of 2018 requires the Departme nt of 
State P olice to create a School Safety Commi ssion to review 
model practices for determining school safety measures and to 
make policy recommendations to the Office of School Safety;  
WHEREAS, Public Act 549 of 2018 requires the Board to 
designate a Di strict empl oyee to act as a liaison to both the 
newly-created Office of School Safety and the School Safety 
Commission; 
WHEREAS, the District’s desig nated liaison to the Office of 
School Safety and School Safety Commission must be a District 
employee wh o regularly a nd continuously works under 
contract for the District; 
WHEREAS, under the Student Safety Act, MCL 752.913(3), the 
Attorney General is resp onsible for operating a hotline that 
allows the public to submit reports regarding potential self-
harm and potential harm or criminal acts directed at school 
students, school employees, or schools in Michigan; and 
WHEREAS, Public Act 670 of 2018 requi res the governing body 
of a school to provide, at least biannually, to the Department 
of State Police curren t emergency contact information for  at 
least 1 school official to ensure that the school official is able 
to receive informat ion submitted through the hotline 
described above.  
NOW THEREFORE LET IT BE RESOLVED:  
1. That the Superintendent is directed to des ignate an 
individual employed by the District or who is assigned to 
regularly and continuously work under contract for the 
District to act as a liaison to the Office of School Safety and 
the School Safety Commission  and the Superintendent 
shall notify the De partment of  State Police of the 
designated liaison by March 28, 2019; and 
2. That the Superintendent is directed to provide t he contact 
Troy Board of Education Meeting 5 March 19, 2019 
12
information of at least one (1)  school official  within the 
District to receive information from the Attorney General’s 
hotline, to the Department of State Police  by March 28, 
2019 as required by Public Act 670. 
Yes: 7 
No: 0

 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Approve the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
 WHEREAS, the School District desires to sell the parcel of land  with the 
following legal description: 
Being port of the Northeast 1/4 of the Southwest 1/4 of Section 23, Town 2 
North, Range 11 East, City of Troy, Oakland County, Michigan, Described as 
beginning at the Northwest corner of Lot 28 "Beaver Run Subdivision", A part of 
the Southwest 1/4 of  Southwest 1/4 Section 23, Town 2 North, Range 11 East, 
City of Troy, Oakland County, Michigan, as recorded in Liber 46, of Pl ats o n 
Page 27, Oakland County records, thence Northe rly 6.00 feet on the extension 
of the Westerly line of Lot  28, thence Easterly 120.00 feet parallel to the 
Northerly line of "Beaver Run Subdivision", thence Southerly 6.00 feet along the 
Northerly extension of the E asterly line of Lot 29, thence Westerly along said 
Northerly subdivision line 120.00 feet to the point of beginning. Cont aining 720 
square feet.  
 And 
WHEREAS, the School District has received an Offer To Purchase this 
property from, Delmar Homes (the “Purchaser”), to purchase the Premises f or 
a purchase price of Ten Thousand and  00/100 ($ 10,000.00) Dollars , (the 
“Purchase Price”); and 
WHEREAS, the School District has reviewed the Offer and is desirous of 
accepting said Offer and authorizing the sale of the Premises on terms 
substantially s imilar to thos e described in the Offer,  which Offer is attached 
hereto and incorporated herein by reference as Exhibit A.   
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
114
  5.D.  4.16.19 
 1. The School District authorizes the sale of the Premises on terms 
substantially similar to the terms contained in the attached Offer. 
2. The Superintendent of the School District, or his designee, is hereby 
authorized to execute the Offer and any and all documents necessary 
and incidental to the sale of the Premises on behalf of the School District. 
3.  The resolution approving the sale of the Boyd St. Property approved by 
the Board of Education at the November 20, 2018 Board of Education 
meeting is hereby rescinded.

E.  CONSIDERATION: Districtwide Site Work Projects 
A RFP was issued to obtain competitive bids for the site work at Costello, 
Hamilton and Martell Elementary Schools and Smith and Larson Middle 
Schools.  Bid responses were reviewed by  Rob Carson, Director of 
Operations and Barton Malow Company, and it is recommended the bid 
be awarded as stated below.  
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
 Be it therefore resolved that the bid for site work projects be awarded 
to Asphalt Specialists, Inc.  in the amount of $822,140.00 and that th is 
amount be approved as an expenditure of the Building and Site Sinking 
Fund and 2016 Capital Projects Fund.

B.  CONSIDERATION: Bathroom Partition Replacement Project 
A RFP was issued to obtain competitive bids for the bathroom partition 
replacement project at Athens, Troy High, Boulan Park and Larson 
Middle Schools.  Bid responses were reviewed by Rob Carson, Director 
of Operations , and it is recommended the bid be awarded as stated 
below.  
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
 Be it therefore resolved  that the bid for the bathroom partition 
replacement project  be awarded to Leggette Co.  in the amount of 
$101,760.00 and that this amount be approved as an expenditure of the 
Building and Site Sinking Fund.

Memorandum 
 TO:  Rick West – Asst. Superintendent of Business 
     Services 
         Operations   
Department 
 FROM:  Rob Carson – Director of Maintenance &  
Operations 
1140 Rankin 
Troy, MI 48083 
Ph: 248.823.4050 
RE:   2019 Toilet Partition Replacement Project RFP 9882 
Fax: 248.823.4077 
DATE:  April 4, 2019 
An RFP was created to obtain competitive bids for a Toilet Partition Replacement Project 
at Athens & Troy High and  Boulan Park & Larson Middle Schools.  The scope of work 
for this RFP is to provide labor & materials to replace the toilet & urinal partitions in 
the Phys. Ed. & Athletics locker rooms at Athens & Troy High and to replace the toilet 
& urinal partitions  in the student/staff bathrooms at Boula n Park & Larson Middle  
Schools as detailed in the bid specifications.   
The RFP resulted in 3 bids with R.E. Leggette Co.  being the apparent low bidder.  A 
post bid interview was performed to confirm bid accuracy , product specifications and 
project timelines.  Please see the attached bid summary detailing the individual bids 
and alternates. 
I am recommending R.E. Leggette Company  for award of this bid and requesting a 
total project cost of $101,760.00.  The funding for this purchase wi ll be from the 
Building Site & Sinking Fund. 
The toilet partition replacement project is scheduled to start June 17, 2019  and be 
completed by August 16, 2019. 
Please let me know if you have any questions.  
Cc:   Pat Orczykowski – Business Department  
 Purchasing Department – File
